        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Vendor hermes-agent as `megaplan/agent/` subpackage (Job A)

## Overview
Vendor the local `hermes-agent` repo into `megaplan` as an in-tree subpackage at `megaplan/agent/` using `git subtree` (preserving history), rewire the five megaplan call sites that import from top-level `run_agent` / `hermes_state` / `hermes_cli`, delete the dead-weight paths listed in the brief, remove `check_hermes_available()` gating, and prove that (a) every megaplan module still imports and (b) `megaplan.agent.run_agent.AIAgent` + `megaplan.agent.hermes_state.SessionDB` resolve. `skills/`, `tools/`, `acp_adapter`, `acp_registry`, `environments/` remain on disk — deferred to Job B; Job A only rewrites their internal absolute imports enough to keep the package importable.

Reachable-surface audit from megaplan side (already done):

| Site | Line | Symbol |
|---|---|---|
| `megaplan/hermes_worker.py` | 26, 36, 290-291 | `AIAgent`, `SessionDB`, `hermes_cli.config.get_env_path` |
| `megaplan/review/parallel.py` | 80-81, 209-210 | `AIAgent`, `SessionDB` |
| `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` | 51-52 | `AIAgent`, `SessionDB` |
| `megaplan/workers.py` | 1580, 1649-1656 | `import run_agent` probe + `check_hermes_available()` |
| `megaplan/_core/io.py` | 270 | `import run_agent` probe |
| `tests/test_parallel_critique.py` | 404-407 | `sys.modules` fakes |
| `tests/test_parallel_review.py` | 198-201, 281-284 | `sys.modules` fakes |
| `README.md` | 23, 25, 83 | install doc |

All hermes imports in megaplan are lazy (inside functions) — `import megaplan.hermes_worker` does not pull hermes runtime deps, which makes the verify step cheap.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Pre-flight — clean tree + snapshot hermes HEAD (`git`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Stash/commit the three dirty files (`megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/prompts/execute_doc.py`, `megaplan/prompts/planning.py`) so subtree merge starts clean.
2. Record `git -C /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent rev-parse HEAD` and its default branch (assume `main`, fall back to `master`). Cite in the merge commit message for provenance.

### Step 2: Subtree-merge hermes-agent at `megaplan/agent/` (`git subtree`)
**Scope:** Small
1. From `megaplan` repo root: `git subtree add --prefix=megaplan/agent /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent main` (no `--squash` — brief says preserve history).
2. Confirm `megaplan/agent/run_agent.py` and `megaplan/agent/hermes_state.py` exist.
3. Ensure `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` is present (empty for Job A).

### Step 3: Map reachable-module surface inside the vendored tree (`megaplan/agent/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. BFS from `megaplan/agent/run_agent.py` and `megaplan/agent/hermes_state.py` via `grep -nE "^(from|import) " …` until only stdlib/third-party remain. This yields the "must-import-cleanly" set for Job A.
2. Tag transitive edges into `skills/`, `tools/`, `acp_adapter*`, `environments/` — their contents are Job B, but their absolute import paths need rewriting so the package loads.

### Step 4: Rewrite internal imports inside `megaplan/agent/` (`megaplan/agent/**/*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. For every module reachable in Step 3, rewrite absolute sibling imports to the `megaplan.agent.*` namespace:
   - `from run_agent ...` → `from megaplan.agent.run_agent ...`
   - `from hermes_state ...` → `from megaplan.agent.hermes_state ...`
   - `from tools... / skills... / acp_adapter... / acp_registry... / environments...` → prefix with `megaplan.agent.`
   - `import run_agent` → `from megaplan.agent import run_agent`
2. Keep `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` empty (don't re-export) — preserves grep-ability at call sites. Explicit submodule imports in call sites is already megaplan's style.

### Step 5: Rewire megaplan call sites + remove `check_hermes_available` (`megaplan/*.py`, `tests/*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Rewrite to vendored paths:
   - `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:290-291` → `from megaplan.agent.run_agent import AIAgent` / `from megaplan.agent.hermes_state import SessionDB`.
   - `megaplan/review/parallel.py:80-81, 209-210` — same.
   - `megaplan/parallel_critique.py:51-52` — same.
   - `megaplan/workers.py:1580` → `from megaplan.agent import run_agent`.
   - `megaplan/_core/io.py:270` → `from megaplan.agent import run_agent`.
2. Delete `check_hermes_available()` from `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:23-49` (including its `from hermes_cli.config import get_env_path` call). Delete both call sites in `megaplan/workers.py:1649-1658` and collapse the surrounding branch to `raise CliError("agent_not_found", f"Agent '{agent}' not found")`.
3. Update test mocks: `tests/test_parallel_critique.py:404-407` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py:198-201, 281-284` — replace `sys.modules["run_agent"/"hermes_state"]` fakes with `monkeypatch.setattr(megaplan.agent.run_agent, "AIAgent", FakeAIAgent)` (vendored package now resolves normally; swapping the class is cleaner than module fakery).

### Step 6: Delete dead weight (`git rm` within `megaplan/agent/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `git rm -r` the brief's list: `evals/ landingpage/ website/ demo/ assets/ node_modules/ package.json package-lock.json tinker-atropos/ honcho_integration/ mini-swe-agent/ gateway/ cron/ hermes_cli/ cli.py auto_improve/ batch_runner.py rl_cli.py datagen-config-examples/ RELEASE_v*.md setup-hermes.sh cli-config.yaml.example`.
2. For "root *.json benchmark artifacts": enumerate with `git ls-files 'megaplan/agent/*.json'` (non-recursive), eyeball the list, remove only the benchmark outputs — do not recurse.
3. Leave `skills/`, `tools/`, `acp_adapter*`, `environments/` in place (Job B).

### Step 7: Packaging + README (`pyproject.toml`, `README.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Verify `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages = ["megaplan"]` picks up `megaplan/agent` (it does — subpackage). Confirm the `exclude = ["megaplan/cloud/_reference/**"]` pattern doesn't accidentally cover `megaplan/agent`.
2. Update README line 23 from `pip install megaplan-harness hermes-agent` → `pip install megaplan-harness`. Leave the `~/.hermes/.env` note; the `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` env-var path in `megaplan/key_pool.py` still works without the deleted `hermes_cli.config.get_env_path`.
3. Do NOT pull hermes runtime deps into `pyproject.toml` in Job A — deferred (assumption below).

### Step 8: Verify imports and run the suite
**Scope:** Small
1. `python -c "import megaplan.hermes_worker, megaplan.workers, megaplan.review.parallel, megaplan.parallel_critique, megaplan._core.io"` — must succeed with only megaplan's current deps.
2. `python -c "from megaplan.agent.run_agent import AIAgent; from megaplan.agent.hermes_state import SessionDB"` — must succeed once hermes runtime deps are present in the venv. Document any missing deps as a follow-up; do not bail out.
3. `grep -rE "^( *)(from|import) (run_agent|hermes_state|hermes_cli)" megaplan/ tests/` → zero hits outside `megaplan/agent/`.
4. `grep -r "check_hermes_available" megaplan/ tests/` → zero hits.
5. `pytest tests/` — green after test-mock updates in Step 5.
6. `megaplan --help` smoke.

## Execution Order
1. Clean tree + snapshot hermes HEAD (Step 1).
2. Subtree merge (Step 2) — creates vendored files.
3. Map reachable graph (Step 3) — gates the rewrite.
4. Rewrite internal imports (Step 4) — package must be importable before callers rewire.
5. Rewire megaplan call sites + strip gating + fix tests (Step 5).
6. Delete dead weight (Step 6).
7. Packaging + README (Step 7).
8. Validate (Step 8).

## Validation Order
1. Cheap greps (stale import paths, `check_hermes_available`).
2. Bare-Python import probe of megaplan modules.
3. Targeted pytest on the two touched test files.
4. Full `pytest tests/`.
5. `megaplan --help`.
6. (info) Manual hermes-backed phase run after runtime deps are installed.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-032",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname=\"hidden md:block\"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones.",

      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked step 7 against the brief's explicit prompt-builder requirement. the revision fixes the old context leak by introducing a brand-new `heavy_criteria_review_prompt`, but the brief said `_review_prompt()` should stay as-is and still run as the `criteria_verdict` check in heavy mode; v3 solves the problem by replacing that heavy-mode criteria path instead of reusing the baseline review prompt, which is still a material divergence from the requested design.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-047",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: issue hints: the plan now wires `mode=auto|chain|idle`, but the user-requested `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` still have no deployment path into the container. reigh's readme uploads the idea file explicitly before running `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` exits if `/workspace/chain.yaml` is missing, so a plan that only sets these paths in `cloud.yaml` without also staging the files still leaves the requested runner modes incomplete.",

      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing.",

      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the prior missing locations in `megaplan/workers.py` and `tests/test_workers.py` are now covered, but one supporting-infrastructure path is still absent from the steps: the repo-root generated schema copy under `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and the tests that read it directly. without either explicitly regenerating that file or changing those tests to materialize schemas in a temp root, the plan can still leave the runtime-schema copy out of sync with the raw registry.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-035",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan adds _set_active_step and _clear_active_step helpers to handlers.py. _run_worker (handlers.py:135) is modified to accept a `resolved` parameter. but _run_worker is also called directly by handle_critique's sequential fallback at line 801 and 803. these calls currently don't pass `resolved`. the plan's step 2.6 for handle_critique says to set active_step after resolution at line 794 \u2014 but the _run_worker calls at 801/803 would re-resolve the agent internally (since `resolved` isn't passed). this means the agent is resolved twice on the critique sequential path. the double-resolution is wasteful but correct (same result). however, to match the plan's goal of 'resolve_agent_mode() is called once per handler invocation', the resolved tuple should be passed to these _run_worker calls too.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-megaplan-s-planning-20260409-2103"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-048",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: supporting infrastructure is still missing for mode inputs. step 3 ports `chain.yaml.example` into package templates, but step 5's `materialize_deploy_dir()` layout does not include it, step 7 deploy does not upload it or `auto.idea_file`, and the docs/validation steps do not mention any artifact-sync path before boot. that leaves the runner depending on files that the deployment plan never places on the container volume.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",

      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",

        ]
      },

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",

        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

Job A scope: (1) map reachable-module surface from megaplan's hermes_worker integration; (2) git-subtree vendor /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/ at megaplan/agent/ preserving history; (3) rewire megaplan/hermes_worker.py to import from in-tree megaplan.agent package; (4) remove check_hermes_available() gating; (5) delete obvious dead weight (evals/, landingpage/, website/, demo/, assets/, node_modules/, package.json, package-lock.json, tinker-atropos/, honcho_integration/, mini-swe-agent/, gateway/, cron/, hermes_cli/, cli.py, auto_improve/, batch_runner.py, rl_cli.py, datagen-config-examples/, root *.json benchmark artifacts, RELEASE_v*.md, setup-hermes.sh, cli-config.yaml.example); (6) verify megaplan still imports and hermes_worker resolves. Out of scope (Job B later): skills/, tools/ filtering, acp_adapter, acp_registry, environments/.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Vendor hermes-agent as `megaplan/agent/` subpackage (Job A)

## Overview
Vendor the local `hermes-agent` repo into `megaplan` as an in-tree subpackage at `megaplan/agent/` using `git subtree` (history preserved), rewire megaplan's lazy imports from top-level `run_agent` / `hermes_state` to the vendored paths, delete the brief's dead-weight list **minus `hermes_cli/`**, remove the `check_hermes_available()` gating, repoint the sibling-repo fallbacks in `megaplan/key_pool.py` and `megaplan/workers.py`, add hermes runtime deps as an optional `[agent]` extra in `pyproject.toml`, and prove that every megaplan module still imports cleanly.

**Deviations from the brief (both driven by critique evidence, not preference):**
1. **Keep `hermes_cli/`** — `hermes-agent/run_agent.py:50` imports `hermes_cli.env_loader` and `hermes-agent/model_tools.py:181-182` imports `hermes_cli.plugins` at module-load time. Deleting it makes `megaplan.agent.run_agent` unimportable (critique verified via `sys.meta_path` blocking). Trim `hermes_cli/` in Job B alongside the other deferred directories.
2. **Fix two sibling-repo references that the brief did not list** — `megaplan/key_pool.py:62-74` searches `repo_root.parent / "hermes-agent" / "auto_improve" / "api_keys.json"`, and `megaplan/workers.py:1662-1665` tells users to "Install claude, codex, or hermes-agent." Leaving those in place would keep a live dependency on the external checkout, defeating the vendoring goal.

Reachable-surface audit from the megaplan side (already done — all hermes imports are lazy, so `import megaplan.hermes_worker` has no third-party cost):

| Site | Line | Symbol |
|---|---|---|
| `megaplan/hermes_worker.py` | 26, 36, 290-291 | `AIAgent`, `SessionDB`, `hermes_cli.config.get_env_path` (inside removed fn) |
| `megaplan/review/parallel.py` | 80-81, 209-210 | `AIAgent`, `SessionDB` |
| `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` | 51-52 | `AIAgent`, `SessionDB` |
| `megaplan/workers.py` | 1580, 1649-1665 | `import run_agent` probe, `check_hermes_available()`, error text |
| `megaplan/_core/io.py` | 270 | `import run_agent` probe |
| `megaplan/key_pool.py` | 62-74 | sibling-repo `api_keys.json` fallback |
| `tests/test_parallel_critique.py` | 404-407 | `sys.modules` fakes — pattern preserved, only key names change |
| `tests/test_parallel_review.py` | 198-201, 281-284 | `sys.modules` fakes — pattern preserved, only key names change |
| `README.md` | 23, 25, 83 | install doc |
| `pyproject.toml` | 21-23 | runtime deps |

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Pre-flight — clean tree + snapshot hermes HEAD (`git`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Stash or commit the three dirty files (`megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/prompts/execute_doc.py`, `megaplan/prompts/planning.py`) so subtree merge starts clean.
2. Record `git -C /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent rev-parse HEAD` and its default branch (assume `main`, fall back to `master`). Cite the SHA in the merge commit body for provenance.

### Step 2: Subtree-merge hermes-agent at `megaplan/agent/` (`git subtree`)
**Scope:** Small
1. From `megaplan` repo root: `git subtree add --prefix=megaplan/agent /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent main` (no `--squash` — preserves history).
2. Confirm `megaplan/agent/run_agent.py`, `megaplan/agent/hermes_state.py`, `megaplan/agent/hermes_cli/`, and `megaplan/agent/pyproject.toml` are present.
3. Ensure `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` exists (empty — keep call sites using explicit submodule imports).

### Step 3: Map reachable-module surface (`megaplan/agent/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. BFS from `megaplan/agent/run_agent.py` and `megaplan/agent/hermes_state.py`, following `^(from|import) ` lines, until leaves are stdlib or third-party packages. Write the full reachable set to a scratch list — this is the ONLY set Step 4 is allowed to modify.
2. Explicitly mark `megaplan/agent/hermes_cli/env_loader.py` and `megaplan/agent/hermes_cli/plugins.py` as reachable (the flagged evidence). Confirm any other `hermes_cli.*` targets appear in the BFS.
3. Record which reachable modules live under `skills/`, `tools/`, `acp_adapter*`, `acp_registry*`, `environments/` — those files get their imports rewritten but their logic/data stays untouched (Job B).

### Step 4: Rewrite internal imports — BFS output only (`megaplan/agent/**/*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. For each module in the Step 3 reachable set (NOT a blanket sweep), rewrite sibling-absolute imports to the `megaplan.agent.*` namespace:
   - `from run_agent ...` → `from megaplan.agent.run_agent ...`
   - `from hermes_state ...` → `from megaplan.agent.hermes_state ...`
   - `from hermes_cli... / tools... / skills... / acp_adapter... / acp_registry... / environments... ` → prefix with `megaplan.agent.`
   - `import run_agent` → `from megaplan.agent import run_agent`
2. Do NOT modify any file outside the BFS set. Leave unreached modules (including most of `skills/` and `tools/`) byte-identical so Job B has an untouched baseline.
3. Keep `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` empty.

### Step 5: Rewire megaplan call sites + strip `check_hermes_available` (`megaplan/*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Rewrite to vendored paths:
   - `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:290-291` → `from megaplan.agent.run_agent import AIAgent` / `from megaplan.agent.hermes_state import SessionDB`.
   - `megaplan/review/parallel.py:80-81, 209-210` — same.
   - `megaplan/parallel_critique.py:51-52` — same.
   - `megaplan/workers.py:1580` → `from megaplan.agent import run_agent`.
   - `megaplan/_core/io.py:270` → `from megaplan.agent import run_agent`.
2. Delete `check_hermes_available()` from `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:23-49` (along with its `from hermes_cli.config import get_env_path` usage). Delete both call sites in `megaplan/workers.py:1649-1658` and collapse the surrounding branch to `raise CliError("agent_not_found", f"Agent '{agent}' not found")`.
3. **Fix supporting infrastructure** (FLAG-002, all_locations):
   - `megaplan/key_pool.py:62-74` — drop the `repo_root.parent / "hermes-agent" / ...` sibling-repo fallback. Keep the `MEGAPLAN_API_KEYS_PATH` env override and the `repo_root / "auto_improve" / "api_keys.json"` entry (no-op on current repo, still works if a user places a file there). No vendored `auto_improve/` to point at, so search order becomes: env override → repo-root `auto_improve/api_keys.json` → none.
   - `megaplan/workers.py:1662-1665` — change error text from `"Install claude, codex, or hermes-agent."` to `"Install claude or codex. (hermes is vendored in megaplan.agent and requires the [agent] extra.)"`.

### Step 6: Update tests — preserve `sys.modules` fake pattern (`tests/test_parallel_critique.py`, `tests/test_parallel_review.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Keep** the existing `monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, ...)` fake-module pattern — this intentionally decouples the unit tests from hermes's real import-time side effects (flagged by critique). Do NOT swap to `monkeypatch.setattr` on the real vendored modules.
2. Only change the sys.modules keys:
   ```python
   monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "megaplan.agent.run_agent", ModuleType("megaplan.agent.run_agent"))
   monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "megaplan.agent.hermes_state", ModuleType("megaplan.agent.hermes_state"))
   sys.modules["megaplan.agent.run_agent"].AIAgent = FakeAIAgent
   sys.modules["megaplan.agent.hermes_state"].SessionDB = FakeSessionDB
   ```
   Apply to `tests/test_parallel_critique.py:404-407` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py:198-201, 281-284`. The call sites in `megaplan/review/parallel.py` and `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` use function-local imports, so Python resolves them against `sys.modules` before hitting the real vendored code.
3. `megaplan.agent` itself must be importable (empty `__init__.py` is fine) — if it weren't, `from megaplan.agent.run_agent import AIAgent` would fail before sys.modules resolution. Confirm with a quick probe inside the test.

### Step 7: Delete dead weight MINUS `hermes_cli/` (`git rm` inside `megaplan/agent/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `git rm -r` the brief's list **with `hermes_cli/` removed**:
   ```
   evals/ landingpage/ website/ demo/ assets/ node_modules/
   package.json package-lock.json
   tinker-atropos/ honcho_integration/ mini-swe-agent/ gateway/ cron/
   cli.py auto_improve/ batch_runner.py rl_cli.py
   datagen-config-examples/
   RELEASE_v*.md setup-hermes.sh cli-config.yaml.example
   ```
2. Root-level `*.json` benchmark artifacts: enumerate with `git ls-files 'megaplan/agent/*.json'` (non-recursive), inspect, delete only obvious benchmark outputs (`results_*.json`, `*_benchmark.json`, dated run dumps). Do NOT recurse — nested JSON is often config/schema.
3. Leave `skills/`, `tools/`, `acp_adapter*`, `acp_registry*`, `environments/`, `hermes_cli/` in place — all handled in Job B.

### Step 8: Packaging — add hermes runtime deps as `[agent]` extra (`pyproject.toml`, `README.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After the subtree merge, read `megaplan/agent/pyproject.toml` to harvest the real dep list (`openai`, `anthropic`, `python-dotenv`, `fire`, `httpx`, `rich`, `requests`, `jinja2`, `pydantic`, `parallel-web`, `litellm`, and whatever else is listed). Do NOT guess — use what's in the vendored file.
2. Add `[project.optional-dependencies]` to `megaplan/pyproject.toml` (parallel to the existing `dependencies` block at lines 21-23):
   ```toml
   [project.optional-dependencies]
   agent = [
     # copy verbatim from megaplan/agent/pyproject.toml
   ]
   ```
   Baseline `dependencies` stays at `PyYAML>=6.0` so users who only run claude/codex don't pay the install cost.
3. Update `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]`: ensure `megaplan/agent` is packaged (already covered by `packages = ["megaplan"]` since it's a subpackage). Confirm the `exclude = ["megaplan/cloud/_reference/**"]` list doesn't need `megaplan/agent/pyproject.toml` added (consider excluding the vendored `pyproject.toml` to avoid confusing the build tool — test both outcomes).
4. README updates:
   - Line 23: `pip install megaplan-harness hermes-agent` → `pip install 'megaplan-harness[agent]'` (quoted for zsh globbing). Add a one-liner: "`[agent]` installs the vendored hermes runtime; omit it if you only use claude or codex."
   - Lines 25, 83: keep `~/.hermes/.env` guidance — `megaplan/key_pool.py` still reads it directly, unchanged.

### Step 9: Verify imports + run the suite (`bash`, `pytest`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Cheap grep gates:
   - `grep -rE "^( *)(from|import) (run_agent|hermes_state|hermes_cli)" megaplan/ tests/` → zero hits outside `megaplan/agent/`.
   - `grep -r "check_hermes_available" megaplan/ tests/` → zero hits.
   - `grep -r "hermes-agent" megaplan/ tests/ README.md` → only legitimate references (e.g. the `[agent]` extra note in README). No filesystem or install-target references.
2. `python -c "import megaplan.hermes_worker, megaplan.workers, megaplan.review.parallel, megaplan.parallel_critique, megaplan._core.io, megaplan.key_pool"` must exit 0 without hermes runtime deps installed (all hermes imports are lazy).
3. In a venv with `pip install -e '.[agent]'`: `python -c "from megaplan.agent.run_agent import AIAgent; from megaplan.agent.hermes_state import SessionDB"` must exit 0.
4. Targeted: `pytest tests/test_parallel_critique.py tests/test_parallel_review.py -q` — green.
5. Full: `pytest tests/ -q` — green.
6. `megaplan --help` smoke.

## Execution Order
1. Clean tree + snapshot hermes HEAD (Step 1).
2. Subtree merge (Step 2) — creates vendored files.
3. BFS reachable map (Step 3) — scopes Step 4.
4. Internal-imports rewrite (Step 4) — package must import before callers rewire.
5. Rewire megaplan call sites + strip gating + fix key_pool/workers (Step 5).
6. Test-mock key renames (Step 6).
7. Delete dead weight, keep `hermes_cli/` (Step 7).
8. Packaging + README (Step 8).
9. Validate (Step 9).

## Validation Order
1. Cheap greps (stale imports, `check_hermes_available`, `hermes-agent` strings).
2. Bare-Python import probe of megaplan modules (no hermes deps).
3. `[agent]`-extra import probe of vendored modules (with hermes deps).
4. Targeted pytest on the two rewritten test files.
5. Full `pytest tests/`.
6. `megaplan --help`.
7. (info) Manual hermes-backed phase run after install.

I reproduced the failure mode with a minimal package layout: `from agent.prompt_builder ...` inside `megaplan/agent/run_agent.py` fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'agent'`, and `importlib.import_module(\"tools.web_tools\")` fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tools'`.",

        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },

        "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept.         - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.

        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue.         Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Vendor hermes-agent as `megaplan/agent/` subpackage (Job A)

## Overview
Vendor `hermes-agent` into `megaplan` as an in-tree subpackage at `megaplan/agent/` using `git subtree` (history preserved). **Strategy pivot (iter 3):** instead of rewriting every vendored module to the `megaplan.agent.*` namespace (fragile — hermes has dynamic `importlib.import_module("tools.web_tools")` strings), install a 4-line `sys.path` shim in `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` that prepends the vendored directory to `sys.path`. Vendored code keeps its original top-level import style (`from agent.X`, `from tools.X`, `from run_agent import ...`, `importlib.import_module("tools.web_tools")`) — all still resolve, because the shim makes `megaplan/agent/` behave like the hermes-agent repo root for import purposes. Megaplan callers use the SAME short names (`from run_agent import AIAgent`) triggered by a preceding `import megaplan.agent`, preserving single-module-identity and leaving existing `sys.modules["run_agent"]` test fakes untouched.

**Rationale for the shim over a rewrite**
- Critique reproduced `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'agent'` and `No module named 'tools'` with a rewrite-only approach; dynamic imports like `importlib.import_module("tools.web_tools")` (`hermes-agent/model_tools.py:138-170`) are impossible to catch with a static BFS.
- Shim approach touches ONE new file (`megaplan/agent/__init__.py`) and zero vendored files.
- Module-identity: both vendored internals and megaplan callers resolve through the same `sys.modules["run_agent"]` key, so `isinstance(x, SessionDB)` holds across the boundary.
- Test-fake compatibility: the existing `monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "run_agent", …)` fakes in `tests/test_parallel_critique.py` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py` continue to work without any changes, because the keys stay the same.

**Deviations from the brief (unchanged from iter 2)**
1. Keep `megaplan/agent/hermes_cli/` — reachable modules (`run_agent.py:50`, `model_tools.py:181-182`) import it at load time.
2. Fix two sibling-repo references the brief did not list: `megaplan/key_pool.py:62-74` (sibling `auto_improve/api_keys.json` fallback) and `megaplan/workers.py:1662-1665` (error text).

**Tradeoff of the shim (documented, accepted)**
Common top-level names the shim exposes — `utils`, `tools`, `agent`, `skills`, `environments` — shadow identically-named top-level modules that might live elsewhere on `sys.path`. Acceptable because megaplan is invoked as a CLI in its own process and the shim prepends (so hermes wins), and because `sys.path` modification is idempotent and scoped to the `megaplan.agent` package's `__init__`.

### Step 3: Diagnostic-only — enumerate vendored top-level names (`megaplan/agent/`)
**Scope:** Small

Expect names like `run_agent`, `hermes_state`, `hermes_cli`, `agent`, `tools`, `skills`, `model_tools`, `hermes_constants`, `utils`, `toolsets`, `environments`, `acp_adapter`, `acp_registry`, plus stdlib/third-party.

### Step 4: Install the sys.path shim (`megaplan/agent/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Overwrite or create** `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` with (verbatim):
   ```python
   """Vendored hermes-agent subpackage.

   Adds this directory to sys.path so hermes's original top-level imports
   (`from agent.X`, `from tools.X`, `import run_agent`, and dynamic
   `importlib.import_module("tools.web_tools")` calls) resolve without
   rewriting vendored code. Callers in megaplan use short names
   (`from run_agent import AIAgent`) after `import megaplan.agent`.
   """
   import os as _os
   import sys as _sys

   _agent_dir = _os.path.dirname(__file__)
   if _agent_dir not in _sys.path:
       _sys.path.insert(0, _agent_dir)
   ```
2. **Do not touch any other vendored file.** Step 4 is one-file, ~10 lines of code. This replaces the prior plan's blanket internal-imports rewrite.
3. Side effect: first `import megaplan.agent` prepends `megaplan/agent/` to `sys.path`. Subsequent imports are idempotent (`if _agent_dir not in _sys.path`).

2. Root-level `*.json` benchmark artifacts: enumerate non-recursively (`git ls-files 'megaplan/agent/*.json'`), delete only obvious benchmark outputs (`results_*.json`, `*_benchmark.json`, dated run dumps). Don't recurse.
3. Leave `skills/`, `tools/`, `acp_adapter*`, `acp_registry*`, `environments/`, `hermes_cli/` in place — Job B handles filtering.

### Step 8: Packaging — add hermes runtime deps as `[agent]` extra (`pyproject.toml`, `README.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Read `megaplan/agent/pyproject.toml` (present after subtree merge) and harvest its runtime deps verbatim.
2. Add to `megaplan/pyproject.toml`:
   ```toml
   [project.optional-dependencies]
   agent = [
     # copy verbatim from megaplan/agent/pyproject.toml
   ]
   ```
   Baseline `dependencies` stays `["PyYAML>=6.0"]`.
3. Confirm `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages = ["megaplan"]` picks up `megaplan/agent/` (it does — subpackage). Consider whether `megaplan/agent/pyproject.toml` should be excluded from the wheel to avoid build-tool confusion; if hatch errors, add it to the `exclude` list.
4. README updates: line 23 → `pip install 'megaplan-harness[agent]'` (quoted for zsh); one-line note that `[agent]` installs the vendored hermes runtime and is optional for claude/codex-only users. Lines 25 and 83 (`~/.hermes/.env`) unchanged.

### Step 9: Verify (`bash`, `pytest`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Cheap gates:**
   - `grep -r "check_hermes_available" megaplan/ tests/` → zero hits.
   - `grep -n "hermes-agent" megaplan/key_pool.py` → zero hits.
   - `grep -rE 'import_module\("(run_agent|hermes_state|hermes_cli|agent|tools|skills|environments|acp_adapter|acp_registry|model_tools|hermes_constants|utils|toolsets)\.' megaplan/agent/` — matches are expected; confirm the shim's `sys.path` prepend covers every first-segment name.
2. **Bare-Python import probe** (no hermes deps installed):
   ```
   python -c "import megaplan.hermes_worker, megaplan.workers, megaplan.review.parallel, megaplan.parallel_critique, megaplan._core.io, megaplan.key_pool, megaplan.agent"
   ```
   Must exit 0. Importing `megaplan.agent` triggers the shim but loads no hermes deps.
3. **Full-deps smoke** (in a venv with `pip install -e '.[agent]'`):
   ```
   python -c "import megaplan.agent; from run_agent import AIAgent; from hermes_state import SessionDB; a = AIAgent(model='openai/gpt-4o-mini', quiet_mode=True, skip_context_files=True, skip_memory=True, enabled_toolsets=[])"
   ```
   Must exit 0. The `AIAgent(...)` instantiation (not just class import) exercises the dynamic `tools.*` loader — verifies the shim covers dynamic imports, addressing the `scope` flag's concern that a pure import check could miss silent tool-registration failures.
4. **Targeted pytest**: `pytest tests/test_parallel_critique.py tests/test_parallel_review.py -q` — green.
5. **Full suite**: `pytest tests/ -q` — green.
6. **CLI smoke**: `megaplan --help`.
7. **`agent_deps_missing` UX check** (in a venv WITHOUT `[agent]` installed): `megaplan --agent hermes <args>` should raise `CliError("agent_deps_missing", "hermes backend requires: pip install 'megaplan-harness[agent]'")`, not the generic `agent_not_found`.

## Execution Order
1. Clean tree + snapshot hermes HEAD (Step 1).
2. Subtree merge (Step 2).
3. Diagnostic top-level-name enumeration (Step 3).
4. Install sys.path shim in `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` (Step 4).
5. Rewire megaplan call sites + strip `check_hermes_available` + add `agent_deps_missing` branch + fix key_pool/workers error text (Step 5).
6. Confirm tests pass unchanged (Step 6).
7. Delete dead weight (Step 7).
8. Packaging + README (Step 8).
9. Validate (Step 9).

## Validation Order
1. Cheap greps (stale helpers, `hermes-agent` strings, dynamic-import coverage).
2. Bare-Python import probe (no hermes deps).
3. Full-deps `AIAgent(...)` instantiation probe (exercises dynamic tool loader).
4. Targeted pytest on the two parallel test files.
5. Full pytest.
6. `megaplan --help`.
7. `agent_deps_missing` UX check.
8. (info) Manual hermes-backed phase run after `[agent]` install.

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    },

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],

        }
        ```

## Overview
Vendor `hermes-agent` into `megaplan` as an in-tree subpackage at `megaplan/agent/` using `git subtree` (history preserved). Integrate via a `sys.path` prepend shim in `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` so hermes's original top-level imports (`from agent.X`, `from tools.X`, `from run_agent import ...`, dynamic `importlib.import_module("tools.*")`) resolve without modifying any vendored file. Megaplan callers use the SAME short names (`from run_agent import AIAgent`) so vendored internals and megaplan callers share the same `sys.modules` identity, and existing test fakes keep working unchanged.

**Iter-4 refinement: confine the shim to hermes-only paths.** The iter-3 plan added module-top `import megaplan.agent` in `_core/io.py` and `workers.py`. Critique verified this activates the shim during ordinary `megaplan --help` / `import megaplan` because `megaplan/__init__.py:10` pulls in `_core`. The fix: move every shim-trigger `import megaplan.agent` from module-top into the exact same function body as the lazy `from run_agent import ...` / `from hermes_state import ...` line it protects, placed immediately before. The five affected functions (`run_hermes_step`, `_run_check` x2, `_is_agent_available`, `detect_available_agents`) are only called on actual hermes paths, so `sys.path` stays pristine during claude/codex-only flows.

**Deviations from the brief (settled, unchanged)**
1. Keep `megaplan/agent/hermes_cli/` — reachable modules import `hermes_cli.*` at load time.
2. Fix two sibling-repo references the brief did not list: `megaplan/key_pool.py:62-74` (sibling `auto_improve/api_keys.json` fallback) and `megaplan/workers.py:1662-1665` (error text).

**Settled decisions (do not revisit):** shim-over-rewrite strategy, `hermes_cli/` retention, `[agent]` optional extra, key_pool sibling-fallback removal, `sys.modules` fake-pattern preservation in tests, `agent_deps_missing` CliError branch, subtree `--no-squash`, Job-B fence around `skills/tools/acp*/environments/hermes_cli`.

### Step 4: Install the sys.path shim (`megaplan/agent/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Write `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` (verbatim, ~10 lines):
   ```python
   """Vendored hermes-agent subpackage.

   Importing this module prepends its directory to sys.path so hermes's
   original top-level imports (`from agent.X`, `from tools.X`,
   `import run_agent`, `importlib.import_module("tools.web_tools")`) resolve
   without rewriting vendored code. Importers in megaplan trigger this shim
   inside the SAME function body as the lazy hermes import it protects —
   never at module top — so non-hermes paths leave sys.path untouched.
   """
   import os as _os
   import sys as _sys

   _agent_dir = _os.path.dirname(__file__)
   if _agent_dir not in _sys.path:
       _sys.path.insert(0, _agent_dir)
   ```
2. No other file under `megaplan/agent/` is modified in Job A.

   ```
   HOME=$(mktemp -d) python -c "
   import megaplan.agent                  # activate shim
   from run_agent import AIAgent          # class import only — no __init__ side effects
   from hermes_state import SessionDB     # class import only
   import model_tools                     # triggers module-scope importlib.import_module('tools.*') calls
   print('OK')
   "
   ```
   Must exit 0. The `import model_tools` exercises the dynamic `tools.*` loader at module-load time (per critique evidence at `hermes-agent/model_tools.py:138-170`) without instantiating `AIAgent`, so no `~/.hermes/logs/errors.log` touch. This is the hermetic replacement the gate asked for.
5. **Targeted pytest:** `pytest tests/test_parallel_critique.py tests/test_parallel_review.py -q`.
6. **Full suite:** `pytest tests/ -q`.
7. **CLI smoke:** `megaplan --help` — must exit 0 AND (per step 9.2) must not mutate `sys.path`.
8. **`agent_deps_missing` UX check** (venv WITHOUT `[agent]` installed): `megaplan --agent hermes <any-args>` must produce the `agent_deps_missing` `CliError` with the actionable message, not the generic `agent_not_found`.

## Execution Order
1. Clean tree + snapshot hermes HEAD (Step 1).
2. Subtree merge (Step 2).
3. Diagnostic enumeration (Step 3).
4. Install sys.path shim (Step 4).
5. Rewire call sites — function-local shim triggers + delete `check_hermes_available` + `agent_deps_missing` branch + key_pool/workers text fixes (Step 5).
6. Confirm tests pass unchanged (Step 6).
7. Delete dead weight (Step 7).
8. Packaging + README (Step 8).
9. Validate (Step 9).

## Validation Order
1. Cheap greps (no stale helpers, no sibling-repo paths, no module-top shim triggers).
2. Side-effect isolation probe — `import megaplan` must not mutate `sys.path`.
3. Bare-Python megaplan import probe (no hermes deps).
4. Hermetic shim-coverage probe under `HOME=$(mktemp -d)` (with `[agent]` deps).
5. Targeted pytest on the two parallel test files.
6. Full pytest.
7. `megaplan --help` + re-verify no sys.path mutation.
8. `agent_deps_missing` UX check.
9. (info) Manual hermes-backed phase run after `[agent]` install.

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-032",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname=\"hidden md:block\"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones.",

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-035",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan adds _set_active_step and _clear_active_step helpers to handlers.py. _run_worker (handlers.py:135) is modified to accept a `resolved` parameter. but _run_worker is also called directly by handle_critique's sequential fallback at line 801 and 803. these calls currently don't pass `resolved`. the plan's step 2.6 for handle_critique says to set active_step after resolution at line 794 \u2014 but the _run_worker calls at 801/803 would re-resolve the agent internally (since `resolved` isn't passed). this means the agent is resolved twice on the critique sequential path. the double-resolution is wasteful but correct (same result). however, to match the plan's goal of 'resolve_agent_mode() is called once per handler invocation', the resolved tuple should be passed to these _run_worker calls too.",

        "fix-megaplan-s-planning-20260409-2103"
      ]
    },

    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

Analyzing iter 4:

**Trajectory signals**: Score 13.5 → 12.0 → 10.75 → **11.0** (first non-monotonic move). Plan deltas 63.6% → 72.0% → 54.2% (shrinking — edits, not pivots). 8 flags resolved total. **FG-002 now has 2 flags across 5 iters** — the TIEBREAKER threshold is technically met.

**Open blockers**:
- **FLAG-006 / correctness-1 / correctness-2**: The function-local shim trigger in `_core/io.detect_available_agents()` and `workers._is_agent_available("hermes")` still fires on non-hermes flows — both are called from `cli.py:694` (`handle_setup_global`) and `workers.py:1602,1660` (fallback logic) during claude/codex-only runs. ## Overview
Vendor `hermes-agent` into `megaplan` at `megaplan/agent/` via `git subtree` (history preserved). Integrate via a `sys.path` prepend shim in `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` so hermes's original top-level imports (`from agent.X`, `from tools.X`, `from run_agent import ...`, dynamic `importlib.import_module("tools.*")`) resolve without modifying any vendored file. Megaplan callers use the same short names so vendored internals and megaplan callers share one `sys.modules` identity.

**Iter-5 refinement: split availability-check from shim-trigger.** Availability helpers (`_is_agent_available`, `detect_available_agents`) are called from non-hermes flows (`megaplan/cli.py:694` `handle_setup_global`, `megaplan/workers.py:1602` `_runtime_fallback_candidates`, `megaplan/workers.py:1645,1660` `resolve_agent_mode`). If those helpers trigger the shim, `sys.path` mutates on non-hermes paths. Fix per gate prescription: availability helpers use a **filesystem-existence check** — no imports, zero side effects. The `import megaplan.agent` shim trigger lives ONLY inside the three real-execution call sites that actually instantiate hermes. By construction, no availability path can mutate `sys.path`.

**Settled decisions (locked; not revisited):**

- `subtree-no-squash`: preserves history.

### Step 4: Install the sys.path shim (`megaplan/agent/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Write `megaplan/agent/__init__.py` (verbatim, ~10 lines):
   ```python
   """Vendored hermes-agent subpackage.

   Importing this module prepends its directory to sys.path so hermes's
   original top-level imports resolve without rewriting vendored code.
   This shim is activated ONLY inside the three real-execution call sites
   (hermes_worker.run_hermes_step, review/parallel._run_check,
   parallel_critique._run_check) — never from availability helpers or at
   module top — so non-hermes paths leave sys.path untouched.
   """
   import os as _os
   import sys as _sys

   _agent_dir = _os.path.dirname(__file__)
   if _agent_dir not in _sys.path:
       _sys.path.insert(0, _agent_dir)
   ```
2. No other file under `megaplan/agent/` is modified in Job A.

### Step 5: Rewire megaplan — split availability-check (filesystem) from shim-trigger (real execution) + delete `check_hermes_available` + `agent_deps_missing` branch (`megaplan/*.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

**Placement rule (addresses FLAG-006 / correctness-1 / correctness-2):** `import megaplan.agent` appears ONLY inside the three real-execution call sites. Availability helpers use `Path(...).is_file()` — no imports. Module-top placement is forbidden anywhere.

1. **`megaplan/workers.py:1576-1584` — availability check via filesystem (no shim trigger):**
   ```python
   def _is_agent_available(agent: str) -> bool:
       if agent == "hermes":
           from pathlib import Path
           return (Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "agent" / "run_agent.py").is_file()
       return bool(shutil.which(agent))
   ```
   `Path(__file__)` is `.../megaplan/workers.py`, so `.parent / "agent" / "run_agent.py"` resolves to `megaplan/agent/run_agent.py`. No `import megaplan.agent`, no `import run_agent`. Zero sys.path mutation.

2. **`megaplan/_core/io.py:263-274` — same pattern:**
   ```python
   def detect_available_agents() -> list[str]:
       import megaplan._core as _core_pkg
       _shutil_ref = _core_pkg.shutil
       available = [a for a in KNOWN_AGENTS if a != "hermes" and _shutil_ref.which(a)]
       from pathlib import Path
       if (Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "agent" / "run_agent.py").is_file():
           available.append("hermes")
       return available
   ```
   `__file__` is `.../megaplan/_core/io.py`, so `parents[1]` is `megaplan/`. Zero imports of hermes.

3. **`megaplan/hermes_worker.py` in `run_hermes_step` (around line 290) — shim trigger retained:**

5. **Targeted pytest:** `pytest tests/test_parallel_critique.py tests/test_parallel_review.py -q`.
6. **Full suite:** `pytest tests/ -q`.
7. **CLI smoke + isolation:** `megaplan --help` (exit 0). Then re-run the isolation probe in Step 9.2 — `sys.path` must remain clean after the CLI invocation.
8. **`agent_deps_missing` UX check** (venv WITHOUT `[agent]`): `megaplan --agent hermes <args>` must produce the `agent_deps_missing` CliError. Note: the filesystem check in `_is_agent_available("hermes")` returns True because vendored files exist, so this error now comes from the try/except wrapping the `from run_agent import AIAgent` inside `run_hermes_step` (Step 5.7). Validates the end-to-end UX path.

## Execution Order
1. Clean tree + snapshot hermes HEAD (Step 1).
2. Subtree merge (Step 2).
3. Diagnostic enumeration + gateway/cron/honcho reference audit (Step 3).
4. Install sys.path shim (Step 4).
5. Rewire — filesystem availability checks + real-execution shim triggers + delete `check_hermes_available` + `agent_deps_missing` branch (inline and inside `run_hermes_step` try/except) + key_pool/workers text fixes (Step 5).
6. Tests (Step 6) — no changes.
7. Delete dead weight, honoring Step 3.3 retentions (Step 7).
8. Packaging + README (Step 8).
9. Validate (Step 9).

## Validation Order
1. Cheap greps (no stale helpers, no sibling-repo paths, AST-verified shim-trigger placement).
2. Strengthened isolation probe — availability helpers AND `import megaplan` leave `sys.path` unchanged.
3. Bare-Python megaplan import probe (no hermes deps).
4. Hermetic shim-coverage probe under `HOME=$(mktemp -d)` (with `[agent]` deps).
5. Targeted pytest on the two parallel test files.
6. Full pytest.
7. `megaplan --help` + re-verify no sys.path mutation.
8. `agent_deps_missing` UX check (now routes through try/except in `run_hermes_step`).
9. (info) Manual hermes-backed phase run after `[agent]` install.

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",

        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

   ```python
   """Vendored hermes-agent subpackage.

   Importing this module prepends its directory to sys.path so hermes's
   original top-level imports resolve without rewriting vendored code.
   Activated via `megaplan.hermes_worker._import_hermes_runtime()` only.
   """
   import os as _os
   import sys as _sys

   _agent_dir = _os.path.dirname(__file__)
   if _agent_dir not in _sys.path:
       _sys.path.insert(0, _agent_dir)
   ```
